The proofs that
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
satisfies the remaining three distributive law axioms follow similarly (and are somewhat simpler than the proof that (
8.23
) commutes.

By
[
2
, Section 3]
, there is a monad
\mathbb{T}_{*}=(T_{*},\mu^{\mathbb{T}_{*}},\eta^{\mathbb{T}_{*}})
on
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{\mathsf{E}}}_{*}
whose EM category of algebras is equivalent to the EM category of algebras for
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
. In case
\mathsf{E}=\mathsf{Set}
,
\mathbb{T}_{*}
has been described in detail in
[
34
, Section 7]
, and the description of
\mathbb{T}_{*}
given in
[
34
, Corollary 7.43]
for graphical species in
\mathsf{Set}
, holds for graphical species in a general category
\mathsf{E}
with sufficient (co)limits.

Precisely, the endofunctor
T_{*}
is the quotient of
T
given by

(8.25)

T_{*}S_{\lx@sectionsign}=S_{\lx@sectionsign},\text{ and }T_{*}S_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X})

for all pointed graphical species
S_{*}=(S,\epsilon,o)
in
\mathsf{E}
. The unit
\epsilon^{T_{*}S}\overset{\textup{def}}{=}T_{*}(\epsilon)\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to T_{*}S_{\mathbf{2}}
is described by the obvious composite

\textstyle{S_{\lx@sectionsign}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{S_{\mathbf{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\mathbb{T}}S}
\textstyle{TS_{\mathbf{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{*}S_{\mathbf{2}},}

and the contracted unit
o^{T_{*}S}\overset{\textup{def}}{=}T_{*}(o)\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to T_{*}S_{\mathbf{0}}
is described by

\textstyle{S_{\lx@sectionsign}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{o}
\textstyle{S_{\mathbf{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\mathbb{T}}S}
\textstyle{TS_{\mathbf{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{*}S_{\mathbf{0}}.}

In particular,
\epsilon^{T_{*}S}
is defined by maps
S(u^{k})\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to S(\mathcal{L}^{k})
(for
k\geq 1
), and
o^{T_{*}S}
by maps
S(\kappa^{m})\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to S(\mathcal{W}^{m})
,
m\geq 1
and
S(o)\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to S(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}})
such that
o\circ S_{\tau}=o
.

The multiplication
\mu^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}
for
\mathbb{T}_{*}
is the one induced by
\mu^{\mathbb{T}}
, that forgets the pair
(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{\Gamma})
– of an
X
-graph
\mathcal{X}
, and a non-degenerate
\mathcal{X}
-shaped graph of connected graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}
– and replaces it with the colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})
. The unit
\eta^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}
for
\mathbb{T}_{*}
is defined by the maps
S_{X}=S(\mathcal{C}_{X})\mapsto\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X})
.

It is then straightforward to derive the following theorem (that was the main result, Theorem 7.46, of
[
34
]
in the case
\mathsf{E}=\mathsf{Set}
) for general
\mathsf{E}
.

Theorem 8.26
.

The EM category
\mathsf{GS}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
of algebras for
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathsf{MO}
.

Proof.

Let
(A,h)
be a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
-algebra in
\mathsf{E}
. Then
h
induces a
\mathbb{T}
-algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{T}}\colon TA\to A
by restriction, and a
\mathbb{D}
-algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{D}}=h\circ D\eta^{\mathbb{T}}A\colon DA\to A
. In particular,
h^{\mathbb{T}}
equips
A
with the a multiplication
\diamond
and contraction
\zeta
such that
(A,\diamond,\zeta)
is a non-unital modular operad in
\mathsf{E}
, and
h^{\mathbb{D}}
, equips
A
with the structure of a pointed graphical species
A_{*}=(A,\epsilon,o)
in
\mathsf{E}
. To prove that
(A,h)
describes a modular operad, we must show that
\epsilon
is a unit for
\diamond.

This is straightforward since
(A,h)
is a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
algebra and therefore
(A,\epsilon,o)
, together with
h^{\mathbb{T}}
, describe a
\mathbb{T}_{*}
-algebra. In particular,
\epsilon
is a unit for
\diamond
, by (
8.25
).

Conversely, if
(S,\diamond,\zeta,\epsilon)
is a modular operad in
\mathsf{E}
, then
(S,\diamond,\zeta)
defines a
\mathbb{T}
-algebra
p^{\mathbb{T}}\colon TS\to S
by
8.2
, and
(S,\epsilon,\zeta\epsilon)
defines a
\mathbb{D}
-algebra by
Section

8.2
. In particular, since
\epsilon
is a unit for
\diamond
, and
p^{\mathbb{T}}
is induced by iterations of
\diamond
and
\zeta
,
p^{\mathbb{T}}
induces a
\mathbb{T}_{*}
-algebra structure on
(S,\epsilon,\zeta\epsilon)
by (
8.25
), whereby
(S,\diamond,\zeta,\epsilon)
describes an algebra for
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
.
∎

9.
A monad for circuit operads

Despite the similarity between the definitions of the monads
\mathbb{T}
and
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
, it is not possible to modify
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
by replacing
\mathbb{T}
with
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
to obtain a distributive law for
\mathbb{D}
and
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
:
Namely, for all graphical species
S
,
DS_{\mathbf{2}}\cong S_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg S_{\lx@sectionsign}
by definition. Hence there is a canonical morphism

S(\shortmid\amalg\shortmid)=S_{\lx@sectionsign}\times S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to DS_{\mathbf{2}}\times DS_{\mathbf{2}}=DS(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{2}})\to TDS_{\mathbf{2}\amalg\mathbf{2}}.

However, there is, in general, no morphism

(S_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg S_{\lx@sectionsign})\times(S_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg S_{\lx@sectionsign})\to TS_{\mathbf{2}\amalg\mathbf{2}}=DTS_{\mathbf{2}\amalg\mathbf{2}}.

Therefore
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon TD\Rightarrow DT
does not extend to a distributive law
{T^{\times}}D\Rightarrow D{T^{\times}}
.

The problem of finding a circuit operad monad on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
is easily solved by observing that the monad
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
is itself a composite: the free graded monoid monad on
\mathsf{psh}(\Sigma)
induces a monad
\mathbb{L}=(L,\mu^{\mathbb{L}},\eta^{\mathbb{L}})
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
, (that will be described in
Section

9.1
), whose algebras are graphical species in
\mathsf{E}
that are equipped with an external product. Moreover, there is a distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
, and the induced composite monad is
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}=\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}
.

It is then straightforward to check that, for all (unpointed) graphical species
S
in
\mathsf{E}
,
LDTS
describes a circuit operad in
\mathsf{E}
. By
[
8
]
, there is a distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}(\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T})}\colon(DT)L\Rightarrow L(DT)
, and hence a composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
, if and only if there are pairwise distributive laws
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
, and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
, such that the following
‘Yang-Baxter’ diagram
of endofunctors on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
commutes:

(9.1)

\textstyle{DTL\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}}
\textstyle{DLT\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}T}
\textstyle{TDL\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}L}
\scriptstyle{T\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{LDT.}
\textstyle{TLD\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}D}
\textstyle{LTD\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}}

In this section, we construct the monad
\mathbb{L}
and show that there is a distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}:TL\Rightarrow LT
such that the induced composite monad is precisely
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
.

The following section gives a description of the distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}:DL\Rightarrow LD
such that the diagram (
9.1
) commutes, and proves that the EM category of algebras for the composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
is canonically equivalent to the category
{\mathsf{CO}}_{\mathsf{E}}
of circuit operads in
\mathsf{E}
.

9.1.
The free external product monad
\mathbb{L}

For any finite set
X
, recall (
Section

2.2
) that
\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})
is the maximal groupoid in
X/\mathsf{Set_{f}}
whose objects are pairs
(Y,f)
with
f\in\mathsf{Set_{f}}(X,Y)
a morphism of finite sets.

As usual, let
\mathsf{E}
be a category with sufficient (co)limits. The free graded monoid endofunctor
L
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
is given by

(9.2)

\begin{array}[]{lll}LS_{\lx@sectionsign}&=&S_{\lx@sectionsign}\\
LS_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\prod_{y\in Y}S_{f^{-1}(y)}\end{array}

for all finite sets
X
. This underlies a monad
\mathbb{L}=(L,\mu^{\mathbb{L}},\eta^{\mathbb{L}})
with monadic multiplication
\mu^{\mathbb{L}}
induced by concatenation of lists, and monadic unit
\eta^{\mathbb{L}}
induced by inclusion of singleton lists.

The following lemma is immediate:

Lemma 9.3
.

The EM category of algebras for the monad
\mathbb{L}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
is equivalent to the category of graphical species with unital external product in
\mathsf{E}
.

In order to describe a distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow
for
\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{T}
, observe first that
L\subset{T^{\times}}
is a subfunctor since, if
S
is a graphical species in
\mathsf{E}
, and
X
a finite set, then

\begin{array}[]{lll}LS_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\prod_{y\in Y}S_{f^{-1}(y)}\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in{{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}}_{\mathrm{iso}}}S(\mathcal{X})\end{array}

where
{{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}}_{\mathrm{iso}}\subset X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}
is the groupoid of
X
-graphs
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{cor}}
that are disjoint unions of corollas
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{cor}}=\coprod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}_{X_{i}}}
(with
\coprod_{i\in I}X_{i}=X
). In particular, from this point of view, the multiplication
\mu^{\mathbb{L}}
for
\mathbb{L}
is just the restriction of the multiplication
\mu^{{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}}
for
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
(
7.16
) to
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{cor}}
-shaped graphs of disjoint unions of corollas,
and the unit
\eta^{\mathbb{L}}
for
\mathbb{L}
, is the obvious corestriction.

Given a finite set
X
, and a connected
X
-graph
\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}
, let
\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
denote the full subgroupoid of
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}^{(\mathcal{X})}
whose objects are non-degenerate
\mathcal{X}
-shaped graphs of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}
such that, for all
(\mathcal{C}_{X_{b}},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}
,
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C}_{X_{b}},b)\in{{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}}_{\mathrm{iso}}
is a disjoint union of corollas. Then,

\begin{array}[]{lll}TLS_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}}LS(\mathcal{C})\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}\mathrm{lim}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})).\end{array}

Since, for all
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
, the colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})
has the structure of an admissible (in general, not connected)
X
-graph, there is a canonical morphism
TLS_{X}\to\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}}S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
.
And, therefore, since every graph is the coproduct in
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
of its connected components,
S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})=\prod_{k\in K}S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{k})
describes an element of
LTS_{X}
for all
X
-graphs
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\coprod_{k\in K}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{k}
.

Let
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
be the natural transformation so obtained. In other words,
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}
is induced by the canonical morphisms,

\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}\left(\mathrm{colim}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X}))\right)\to\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\ (\text{where }S\in{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}},\text{ and }X\in{\mathbf{B}}),

that forgets pairs

(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{\Gamma})
where
\mathcal{X}
is a connected admissible
X
-graph and
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
, and remembers only the
X
-graphs given by the colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})=\coprod_{k\in K}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})_{k}
, in the evaluation of
S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X}))
.

Lemma 9.4
.

The natural transformation
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
satisfies the four axioms of a distributive law, and
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}={\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
. Hence, algebras for
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}
are non-unital circuit operads.

Proof.

Since a graph is the disjoint union of its connected components,
LT={T^{\times}}\colon{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}\to{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
.

Moreover,
L\subset{T^{\times}}
and
T\subset{T^{\times}}
so
TL\subset({{T^{\times}}})^{2}
is a subfunctor, and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}
is just the restriction to
TL
of
\mu^{{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}}\colon({{T^{\times}}})^{2}\Rightarrow{T^{\times}}=LT
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
. The four distributive law axioms then follow immediately from the monad axioms.
∎

9.2.
An iterated distributive law for circuit operads

So far, we have defined three monads
\mathbb{T},\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{L}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
, and two distributive laws
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon TD\Rightarrow DT
and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
.

Observe also that there is an obvious choice of natural transformation
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
that defines the distributive law for
\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{D}
: By definition of
D
, we may extend the natural transformation

L\eta^{\mathbb{D}}\colon L\Rightarrow LD
to a natural transformation
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
by

S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to DS_{\mathbf{2}}\xrightarrow{\eta^{\mathbb{L}}DS}LDS_{\mathbf{2}},\ \text{ and }S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to DS_{\mathbf{0}}\xrightarrow{\eta^{\mathbb{L}}DS}LDS_{\mathbf{0}},\ \text{ for all }S\in{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}.

Informally,
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}
takes a tuple
(S_{X_{i}})_{i=1}^{n}
of objects in the image of
S
, and regards it as a tuple in the image of
DS
, and takes the image of
S_{\lx@sectionsign}
in
DS_{\mathbf{0}}
or
DS_{\mathbf{2}}
and regards it as a 1-tuple in the image of
DS
.

This construction is natural in
S
, and it is simple to show that
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}
so obtained describes a distributive law for
\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{D}
.

It remains to show that there is an iterated distributive law for the three monads, and that the resulting composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
is the desired circuit operad monad.

Observe first that, for any graph
\mathcal{G}
and any subset
W\subset V_{2}
of bivalent vertices of
\mathcal{G}
, there is a canonical full embedding
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}^{(\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W})}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Gr_{et}}^{(\mathcal{G})}
of categories of non-degenerate graphs of graphs, that preserves similarity classes of colimits:

Namely, if
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W})}\to\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
is a non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W}
-shaped graph of graphs, then there is an induced non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
defined by

(9.5)

(\mathcal{C},b)\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{2}}&(\mathcal{C},b)\text{ is a neighbourhood of }v\in W\\
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathsf{del}_{\setminus W}\circ b)&\text{ otherwise,}\end{array}\right.

and such that
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}
induces an inclusion
W\hookrightarrow V_{2}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X}))
(see
Figure

21
), and

(9.6)

\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})_{\setminus W}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}).

\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}
\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W}
\mathcal{G}

Figure 21.
The colimits of the
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}
described in (
9.5
), and the
\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}
are similar in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
.

In particular, the embedding
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}^{(\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W})}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Gr_{et}}^{(\mathcal{G})}
restricts to an inclusion
\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W})}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{G})}
.

This will be used to prove:

Proposition 9.7
.

The triple of distributive laws
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon TD\Rightarrow DT
,
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
, and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
on
\mathsf{GS}
, describe a composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
.

Proof.

By
[
8
, Theorem 1.6]
, we must check that the diagram (
9.1
) commutes for
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}},\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}
, and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}
:

For all graphical species
S
in
\mathsf{E}
and all finite sets
X
,

TDLS_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}\coprod_{W\subset(V_{0}\amalg V_{2})(\mathcal{X})}\mathrm{lim}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})}}}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))

is indexed by triples

(\mathcal{X},W,\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W})
, where
\mathcal{X}
is a connected
X
-graph,
W\subset V_{2}(\mathcal{X})
(or
\mathcal{X}\cong\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
and
W=V(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}})
) and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}
is a non-degenerate
\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}
-shaped graph of graphs such that
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(b)
is a disjoint union of corollas for each
(\mathcal{C}_{X_{b}},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})}
.

Observe first that, if
W=V(\mathcal{X})
, then either
\mathcal{X}\cong\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
or
\mathcal{X}\cong\mathcal{W}^{m}
in
\mathbf{0}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}
, or
\mathcal{X}\cong\mathcal{L}^{k}
in
\mathbf{2}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}
. In each case
\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}\cong(\shortmid)
and hence
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}
is the trivial graph of graphs
id\mapsto(\shortmid)
. In particular, the maps
S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\to TDLS_{X}\to LDTS_{X}
induced by each path
TDL\Rightarrow LDT
in (
9.1
) coincide, since both factor through
S_{\lx@sectionsign}
.

Assume therefore that
W\neq V(\mathcal{X})
. The two paths in (
9.1
) are then described by the following data:

Top:

\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W},\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}),\\
\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})\\
\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L\eta^{\mathbb{D}}T}
\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathcal{X},W,\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}),\\

\scriptstyle{\tiny{\text{delete }W\text{ in }\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})\\
\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{DTLS_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}S}
\textstyle{DLTS_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where, in each case, we evaluate

and take the appropriate colimit of such objects.

Bottom:

The bottom path of (
9.1
) gives

\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathcal{X},W,\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}),\\

where

is the non-degenerate
\mathcal{X}
-shaped graph of graphs in

obtained from

).

So,
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})=\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})_{\setminus W}
by (
9.6
). Hence, the diagram (
9.1
) commutes, and
(\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}},\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}},\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}})
describe an iterated distributive law for
\mathbb{L},\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{T}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
.
∎

It therefore remains to prove:

Theorem 9.8
.

The EM category of algebras for the composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
is isomorphic to the category
{\mathsf{CO}}_{\mathsf{E}}
of circuit operads in
\mathsf{E}
.

Proof.

By
[
2
]
, for a composite monad is equipped with an algebra structure for each of the component monads. Hence, an algebra
(A,h)
for the composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}
has an
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}
-algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon LTA\to A
, and also a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon DTA\to A
. Each structure is given by the corresponding restriction
LTA\to LDTA\xrightarrow{h}A
, respectively
DTA\to LDTA\xrightarrow{h}A
. Therefore, by
9.4
,
h
induces an external product
\boxtimes
, and contraction
\zeta
on
A
such that
(A,\boxtimes,\zeta)
is a non-unital circuit operad. And, by
8.26
,
h
induces a unital multiplication
(\diamond,\epsilon)
on
A
, such that

\diamond_{X,Y}^{x\ddagger y}=\zeta_{X\amalg Y}^{x\ddagger y}\boxtimes_{X,Y}\colon(A_{X}\times A_{Y})^{x\ddagger y}\to A_{X\amalg Y\setminus\{x,y\}}.

Hence,

\textstyle{A_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(id\times ch_{x})\circ\Delta}
\textstyle{(A_{X}\times A_{\lx@sectionsign})^{x\ddagger 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{id\times\epsilon}
\textstyle{(A_{X}\times A_{\mathbf{2}})^{x\ddagger 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta^{x\ddagger 2}_{X\amalg\mathbf{2}}\circ\boxtimes}
\textstyle{A_{X}}

is the identity on
A_{X}
. And therefore,
(A,h))
has a canonical circuit operad structure.

Conversely, a circuit operad
(S,\boxtimes,\zeta,\epsilon)
in
\mathsf{E}
has an underlying modular operad structure
(S,\diamond,\zeta,\epsilon)
, where
\diamond_{X,Y}^{x\ddagger y}=\zeta_{X\amalg Y}^{x\ddagger y}\boxtimes_{X,Y}
and hence
(S,\boxtimes,\zeta,\epsilon)
defines a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
-algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon DTS\to S
by
8.26
. By
9.3
,
(S,\boxtimes)
has an
\mathbb{L}
-algebra structure where
h^{\mathbb{L}}\colon LS\to S
is given by
\boxtimes_{X,Y}\colon S_{X}\times S_{Y}
on
S_{X}\times S_{Y}
. It follows immediately from the circuit operad axioms that
h^{\mathbb{L}}\circ Lh^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon LDTS\to S
gives
S
the structure of an
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
-algebra.

These maps are functorial by construction and extend to an equivalence
{\mathsf{CO}}_{\mathsf{E}}\simeq{\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
.∎

Theorem 9.9
.

For each palette
(\mathfrak{C},\omega)
, there is an equivalence of the categories
{\mathsf{CA}}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}\simeq{\mathsf{CO}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}}
of
(\mathfrak{C},\omega)
-coloured circuit algebras and circuit operads in
\mathsf{Set}
. This extends to an equivalence
{\mathsf{CA}}\simeq\mathsf{CO}
of the categories of all circuit algebras and all circuit operads in
\mathsf{Set}
.

Proof.

By
5.21
, there is a faithful functor
{\mathsf{CA}}\to\mathsf{CO}
, that restricts to a functor
{\mathsf{CA}}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}\to{\mathsf{CO}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}}
for all palettes
(\mathfrak{C},\omega)
.

For the converse, let
(A,h)
be an algebra for
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
, with
(A_{\lx@sectionsign},A_{\tau})=(\mathfrak{C},\omega)
, and let
(A,\boxtimes,\zeta,\epsilon)
be the underlying circuit operad, with contracted unit
o=\zeta\circ\epsilon\colon\mathfrak{C}\to A_{\mathbf{0}}
.

Let
\bm{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{n})\in\mathfrak{C}^{\mathbf{n}}
, and, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and each
1\leq i\leq k
,
\bm{c}_{i}=(c_{i,1},\dots,c_{i,m_{i}})\in\mathfrak{C}^{\mathbf{m}_{i}}
, be elements of
\small{\mathsf{list}}\mathfrak{C}
, and let
(\overline{f},\lambda)
in
\mathrm{WD}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}(\bm{c}_{1},\dots,\bm{c}_{k};\bm{d})
be a wiring diagram with underlying uncoloured Brauer diagram
f=(\tau,\mathrm{k})\in\mathsf{BD}(\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i},n)
.

By (
6.1
), the open part
(\tau,0)
of
\overline{f}
describes an
\mathbf{n}
-graph
\mathring{\mathcal{X}}=\mathring{\mathcal{X}}(\overline{f})
with
k
ordered vertices
(v_{i})_{i=1}^{k}
and a distinguished étale neighbourhood
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}_{i}},b_{i})\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathring{\mathcal{X}})}
of each
v_{i}
.
Moreover, the colouring
\lambda
of
\overline{f}
describes an involution-preserving map
{{\lambda}_{\partial}}\colon E(\mathring{\mathcal{X}})\to\mathfrak{C}
, such
j\mapsto d_{j}
for each port
1\leq j\leq n
of
\mathring{\mathcal{X}}
, and
b_{i}(l_{i})\mapsto c_{i,l_{i}}
for each port
1\leq l_{i}\leq m_{i}
of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}_{i}}
,
1\leq i\leq k
.

So, for any
k
-tuple
(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{k})\in A_{\bm{c}_{1}}\times\dots\times A_{\bm{c}_{k}}
there is an element
\alpha\in A(\mathring{\mathcal{X}})
defined by
A(b_{i})(\alpha)=\phi_{i}\in A(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}_{i}})
for each
v_{i}
, and
A(ch_{e})(\alpha)={{\lambda}_{\partial}}(e)
for each edge
e\in E(\mathring{\mathcal{X}})
. Since
LTA\subset LDTA
, the assignment

(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{k})\mapsto\alpha\in A(\mathring{\mathcal{X}})
extends to a map

A(\mathring{{f}},\lambda)\colon A_{\bm{c}_{1}}\times\dots\times A_{\bm{c}_{k}}\to A(\mathring{\mathcal{X}})\to LTA_{\mathbf{n}}\to LDTA_{\mathbf{n}}\xrightarrow{h}A_{\mathbf{n}}.

Moreover, the restriction of
{\widetilde{\lambda}}\colon\pi_{0}(f)\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}
to the set of
\mathrm{k}
closed components of
f
defines a
\mathrm{k}
-tuple
(\tilde{c}^{1},\dots,\tilde{c}^{\mathrm{k}})
in
{\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}}
, and hence an element

o(\mathrm{k},{\widetilde{\lambda}})\overset{\textup{def}}{=}o_{\tilde{c}^{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes o_{\tilde{c}^{\mathrm{k}}}\in A_{\mathbf{0}}.

Hence, there is a morphism of sets
A(\overline{f},\lambda)\colon A_{\bm{c}_{1}}\times\dots\times A_{\bm{c}_{k}}\to A_{\bm{d}}
given by

(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{k})\mapsto\left(A(\overline{\mathring{{f}}})(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{k})\right)\boxtimes o(\mathrm{k},{\widetilde{\lambda}})\in A(\bm{d}).

Let
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}(\overline{f})\overset{\textup{def}}{=}\mathring{\mathcal{X}}\amalg\mathcal{W}\amalg\dots\amalg\mathcal{W}
be the disjoint union of
\mathring{\mathcal{X}}
with
\mathrm{k}
copies of the wheel graph
\mathcal{W}
. Since
o_{\tilde{c}}=h(A(\kappa)(c))
for all
c\in\mathfrak{C}
,

o(\mathrm{k},{\widetilde{\lambda}})=h\left(A(\kappa\amalg\dots\amalg\kappa)(c^{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes c^{\mathrm{k}})\right)\text{ with }A(\kappa\amalg\dots\amalg\kappa)(c^{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes c^{\mathrm{k}})\in A(\mathcal{W}\amalg\dots\amalg\mathcal{W}).

Therefore
A(\overline{f},\lambda)
is precisely the composite

A_{\bm{c}_{1}}\times\dots\times A_{\bm{c}_{k}}\xrightarrow{A(\mathring{{f}},\lambda)\amalg\left(\coprod_{i=1}^{\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{W}(e_{c^{i}})\right)}A(\mathcal{X})\longrightarrow LDT(A)_{\mathbf{n}}\xrightarrow{h}A_{\mathbf{n}}

that restricts to
A(\mathring{{f}},\lambda)
on
A(\mathring{\mathcal{X}})
.

The distinguished element
\cup_{c}\in\mathrm{WD}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}((\omega c,c);\varnothing_{\mathfrak{C}})
has
\mathcal{X}(\cup_{c})=\mathcal{W}
, and induces a morphism
A({\cup_{c}})\colon A_{(\omega c,c)}\to A_{\mathbf{0}}
by

A_{\cup_{c}}\colon A_{(\omega c,c)}\longrightarrow A(\mathcal{W})\longrightarrow LDTA(\mathcal{W})\xrightarrow{h}A_{\mathbf{0}}.

Slightly more care is needed for
\cap_{c}\in\mathrm{WD}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}(-;(c,\omega c))
, with
\mathcal{X}(\cap_{c})=(\shortmid)
. Then, the morphism
A(\cap_{c})\colon I\to A_{(c,\omega c)}
is induced by
c\in A(\shortmid)\xrightarrow{\epsilon_{c}}A_{\mathbf{2}}
.

If
(\overline{f},\lambda)\in\mathrm{WD}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}(\bm{c}_{1},\dots,\bm{c}_{k};\bm{d})
and
\mathring{\mathcal{X}}
are as before, let
(\overline{f}^{i},\lambda^{i})\in\mathrm{WD}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}(\bm{b}_{i,1},\dots,\bm{b}_{i,k_{i}};\bm{c}_{i})
with
\bm{b}_{i,j}=(b_{i,j,1},\dots,b_{i,j,K_{i,j}})\in\mathfrak{C}^{K_{i,j}}
, for
1\leq i\leq k
,
1\leq j\leq k_{i}
.

For each
1\leq i\leq k
, let

A(\overline{f}^{i},\lambda^{i})\colon A_{\bm{b}_{i,1}}\times\dots\times A_{\bm{b}_{i,k_{i}}}\to A(\mathcal{X}^{i})\to LT(A)_{\mathbf{m}_{i}}\to LDT(A)_{\mathbf{m}_{i}}\xrightarrow{h}A_{\mathbf{m}_{i}}

be the induced morphism with image in
A_{\bm{c}_{i}}\subset A_{\mathbf{m}_{i}}
.
Then the collection of composable wiring diagrams
\left((\overline{f},\lambda),(\overline{f}^{i},\lambda^{i})_{i}\right)
, describes an
\mathcal{X}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}\to\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
,
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}_{i}},b^{i})\mapsto\mathcal{X}^{i}=\mathcal{X}(\overline{f}^{i})
(that is non-degenerate if and only if none of the graphs
\mathcal{X}^{i}
have stick components). By the monad algebra axioms, and composing with
\epsilon
where necessary,
\mathbf{\Gamma}
induces an element of
(LDT)^{2}A_{\bm{d}}
. Hence there are morphisms

A\left(\gamma(\overline{f},\lambda),(\overline{f}^{i},\lambda^{i})_{i}\right)\colon\prod_{i=1}^{k}\prod_{j=1}^{k_{i}}A_{\bm{b}_{i,j}}\longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{k}A_{\bm{c}_{i}}\longrightarrow A_{\bm{d}}.

In particular, the composition
\bigcirc_{\tilde{c}}=\cup_{c}\circ\cap_{\omega c}
corresponds to the morphism
I\to A_{(c,\omega c)}\to A_{\mathbf{0}}
given by
o_{\tilde{c}}=\zeta\epsilon_{c}\colon I\to A_{(c,\omega c)}\to A_{\mathbf{0}}
.

The monad algebra axioms, and the definition of the distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon TD\Rightarrow DT
, imply that this defines a circuit algebra structure on the collection
(A_{\bm{c}})_{\bm{c}\in\mathfrak{C}}
.

It is straightforward to verify that this assignment is natural in
(A,h)
, and hence extends to a functor
\mathsf{CO}\to{\mathsf{CA}}
such that, moreover, the pair of functors
{\mathsf{CA}}\leftrightarrows\mathsf{CO}
in fact define an (adjoint) equivalence of categories.
∎

10.
A nerve theorem for circuit operads

This section recalls the nerve theorem for
\mathsf{Set}
-valued modular operads
[
34
, Theorem 8.2]
, and gives a proof, by the same methods and using
9.9
, of an analogous result for
\mathsf{Set}
-valued circuit algebras.

10.1.
Overview of the method

Let
\Xi
be the category, whose objects are indexed by connected graphs, obtained in bo-ff factorisation of the functor
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}\to\mathsf{GS}\to\mathsf{MO}
. Let
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
be the full subcategory of connected graphs and pointed étale morphisms from
Section

8.3
, that is obtained in the bo-ff factorisation of the functor
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}\to\mathsf{GS}\to\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
, and, as in
Section

8.4
, let
\mathbb{T}_{*}
be the monad on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
whose EM category of algebras is equivalent to the category
\mathsf{MO}
of modular operads (in
\mathsf{Set}
).

So, there is a commuting diagram of functors

(10.1)

\textstyle{\Xi\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\textstyle{\mathsf{MO}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{forget}^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
\scriptstyle{N}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\Xi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\textstyle{\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\scriptstyle{j}
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{forget}^{\mathbb{D}}}

f.f.
\scriptstyle{\text{free}^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{GS}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

f.f.
\scriptstyle{\text{free}^{\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}).}

In
[
34
, Section 8]
, it was shown that that, though the modular operad monad
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}
does not, itself, satisfy the conditions of
[
3
, Sections 1 & 2]
, the monad
\mathbb{T}_{*}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
does, whereby the following theorem holds:

Theorem 10.2
.

[
34
, Theorem 8.2]

The monad
\mathbb{T}_{*}
, and the category
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
, satisfy the conditions of
[
3
]
(
\mathbb{T}_{*}
has arities
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
). Hence, the nerve
N\colon\mathsf{MO}\to\mathsf{psh}(\Xi)
is fully faithful.
Its essential image consists of those presheaves
P
on
\Xi
such that

P(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C})\text{ for all graphs }\mathcal{G}.

The abstract nerve theory conditions of
[
3
]
are also not satisfied by the the circuit operad monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}
, and the aim of this section is to modify the proof of
10.2

to the circuit operad case.

By (
2.5
) there exists a monad
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
, and
a distributive law
T_{*}\widetilde{L}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}
for the monads
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}
and
\mathbb{T}_{*}
, such that the EM category of algebras for the composite
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
is canonically isomorphic to the EM category of algebras for
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}
, which itself is just the category
\mathsf{CO}\simeq{\mathsf{CA}}
of circuit operads in
\mathsf{Set}
.

The idea now is to show that
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
together with a suitable subcategory of
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
, satisfies the conditions of
[
3
, Sections 1 & 2]
and hence that the induced nerve is fully faithful.

Let
{\Xi^{\times}}
be the category obtained in the bo-ff factorisation of
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}\to\mathsf{GS}\to\mathsf{CO}
. By
Section

8.3
(see also
[
34
, Section 7.3]
), the full subcategory
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\subset\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
of all graphs and pointed morphisms induces a fully faithful inclusion
\mathsf{GS}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{Gr}_{*})
.

Hence, we may consider the following diagram of functors in which the left and middle squares commute, and the right squares commute up to natural isomorphism:

(10.3)

\textstyle{{{\Xi^{\times}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\textstyle{\mathsf{MO}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{forget}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
\scriptstyle{N_{{{\Xi^{\times}}}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}({{\Xi^{\times}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\textstyle{\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\scriptstyle{j}
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{forget}^{\mathbb{D}}}

f.f.
\scriptstyle{\text{free}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{Gr}_{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
dense
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{Gr_{et}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{GS}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

f.f.
\scriptstyle{\text{free}^{\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}).}

The remainder of this paper is dedicated to proving the following theorem using a modification of the proof of
10.2
(
[
34
, Theorem 8.2]
):

Theorem 10.4
.

The functor
N\colon\mathsf{CO}\to\mathsf{psh}({\Xi^{\times}})
is full and faithful. Its essential image consists of precisely those presheaves
P
on
{\Xi^{\times}}
whose restriction to
\mathsf{psh}(\Xi)
are graphical species. In other words,

(10.5)

P(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C})\text{ for all graphs }\mathcal{G}.

Remark 10.6
.

Unlike the operad case (
10.2
), the category
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\subset\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
of connected graphs and pointed morphisms cannot be used to prove an abstract nerve theorem for circuit operads (see
10.17
).

10.2.
The composite monad
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}
on
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{\mathsf{E}}}_{*}

The first step is to understand the monad
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}
on
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{\mathsf{E}}}_{*}
. Here
\mathsf{E}
is, as usual, an arbitrary category with sufficient limits and colimits. From
Section

10.3
, we will restrict to the case
\mathsf{E}=\mathsf{Set}
.

For all pointed graphical species
S_{*}=(S,\epsilon,o)
in
\mathsf{E}
, the lift
\widetilde{L}(S_{*})
is given by
\widetilde{L}(S_{*})=(LS,\eta^{\mathbb{L}}\epsilon,\eta^{\mathbb{L}}o)
where
\mu^{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}},\eta^{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}}
are induced by
\mu^{\mathbb{L}}
and
\eta^{\mathbb{L}}
in the obvious way.

The categories
X\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
of similar connected
X
-graphs were defined in
Section

8.4
. Similarity morphisms preserve connected components, and hence the category
X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
of
all

X
-graphs and similarity morphisms, may be defined as follows:

(10.7)

X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}\overset{\textup{def}}{=}\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\coprod_{y\in Y}\left(f^{-1}(y)\right)\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}.

The monad
\mathbb{T}_{*}=(T_{*},\mu^{\mathbb{T}_{*}},\eta^{\mathbb{T}_{*}})
with
T_{*}S_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X})
was discussed in
Section

8.4
.

Let
S_{*}=(S,\epsilon,o)
be a pointed graphical species in
\mathsf{E}
and let
X
be a finite set. Then, the endofunctor
{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}
admits is described by the obvious quotient of
{T^{\times}}
:

(10.8)

\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}}S_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\left(\prod_{y\in Y}T_{*}S_{f^{-1}(y)}\right)\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\left(\prod_{y\in Y}\left(\mathrm{colim}_{(\mathcal{G},\rho)\in(f^{-1}(y))\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{G})\right)\right)\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X}).\par\end{array}

And,

\begin{array}[]{lll}T_{*}\tilde{L}S_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}}LS(\mathcal{C})\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}\mathrm{lim}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})).\end{array}

The distributive law
T_{*}\widetilde{L}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}
is induced by the distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT={T^{\times}}
, which is compatible with similarity morphisms by construction: So, for each finite set
X
, a colimit of
S
indexed by pairs
(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{\Gamma})
of a connected admissible
X
-graph
\mathcal{X}
, and
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
, is replaced by the colimit indexed over
(in general not connected) admissible
X
-graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})
.

10.3.
A graphical category for circuit operads

Assume
\mathsf{E}=\mathsf{Set}
for the remainder of the paper.

The category
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
obtained in the bo-ff factorisation of
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}\to\mathsf{GS}\to\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
has been discussed in
Section

8.3
. Let
{\Xi^{\times}}
be the category obtained in the bo-ff factorisation of
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}\to\mathsf{GS}\to\mathsf{CO}
(and
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\to\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\to\mathsf{CO}
). This is the full subcategory of the Kleisli category
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
of
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
on free circuit operads of the form
{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{G}=LDT\Upsilon\mathcal{G}
for graphs
\mathcal{G}\in\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
. So, we may regard objects of
{\Xi^{\times}}
as graphs. Morphisms
\beta\in{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
are given by morphisms in
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}({\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{G},{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H})
.

To understand
{\Xi^{\times}}
, the first step is therefore to describe
{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H}
for all graphs
\mathcal{H}
.

Since each connected component of
X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
contains an (in general, not connected) admissible
X
-graph
\mathcal{X}
, it follows from (
10.8
) that, for all finite sets
X
, elements of
({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H})_{X}\cong{\Xi^{\times}}({\mathcal{C}_{X}},\mathcal{H})
are represented by pairs
(\mathcal{X},f)
where
\mathcal{X}
is an admissible
X
-graph and
f\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H})
. In particular,
f
factors uniquely as a morphism
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W_{f}}
in
\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
followed by a morphism
f^{\bot}\in\mathsf{Gr_{et}}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W_{f}},\mathcal{H})
.
Pairs
(\mathcal{X}^{1},f^{1})
and
(\mathcal{X}^{1},f^{2})
represent the same element
[\mathcal{X},f]_{*}\in({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H})_{X}
if and only if there is an object
\mathcal{X}_{f}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
and a commuting diagram

(10.9)

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{1}}
\scriptstyle{f^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}^{f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\bot}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{2}}
\scriptstyle{f^{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}}

in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
such that
g^{j}
are similarity morphisms in
X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
for
j=1,2
, and
f^{\bot}\in\mathsf{Gr_{et}}(\mathcal{X}^{f},\mathcal{H})
is an (unpointed) étale morphism.

In particular, for all
\mathcal{H}
,
e\in E(\mathcal{H})
, and all
m\geq 1
, the following special case of (
10.9
) commutes in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
:

(10.10)

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{z}
\scriptstyle{ch_{e}\circ z}
\textstyle{(\shortmid)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{ch_{e}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}^{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\scriptstyle{ch_{e}\circ\kappa}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}.}

Since
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})\cong{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{C})
, morphisms
\gamma\in{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
are represented by pairs
(\mathbf{\Gamma},f)
, where
\mathbf{\Gamma}:\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
is a non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs, and
f\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}),\mathcal{H})
is a morphism in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
(see
[
34
, Section 8.1]
).

The following lemma generalises
[
34
, Lemma 8.9]
, and is proved in exactly the same manner.

Lemma 10.11
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
be graphs and, for
i=1,2
, let
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{i}
be a non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs with colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{i}(\mathcal{G})
, and
f^{i}\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{i}(\mathcal{G}),\mathcal{H})
.
Then
(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1},f^{1}),(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2},f^{2})
represent the same element
\gamma
of
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
if and only if there is a representative
(\mathbf{\Gamma},f)
of
\gamma
, and a commuting diagram in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
of the following form:

(10.12)

\textstyle{\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H},}

where the morphisms in the top row are vertex deletion morphisms (including
z\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\to(\shortmid)
) in
\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
, and
f\in\mathsf{Gr_{et}}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}),\mathcal{H})
.

The following terminology is from
[
22
]
.

Definition 10.13
.

A
(pointed) free morphism in
{\Xi^{\times}}
is a morphism in the image of the inclusion
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow{\Xi^{\times}}
. An
unpointed free morphism
in
{\Xi^{\times}}
is a morphism in the image of
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow{\Xi^{\times}}
. A
generic morphism
[\mathbf{\Gamma}]
in
{\Xi^{\times}}
is a morphism in
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
with a representative of the form
(\mathbf{\Gamma},id_{\mathcal{H}})
.

So, a free morphism in
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
has a representative of the form
(id,f)
where
id\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
is the trivial identity
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs with colimit
\mathcal{G}
and
f\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
. Generic morphisms
[\mathbf{\Gamma}]
in
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
are described by non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}
with colimit
\mathcal{H}
, and hence induce isomorphisms
E_{0}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\cong}E_{0}(\mathcal{H})
on boundaries.

In particular, morphisms in
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
factor as
\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{[\mathbf{\Gamma}]}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{del}_{\setminus W}}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})_{\setminus W}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{H}
. Here
\mathbf{\Gamma}\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
is a non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs with colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})\in\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
,
and
f\in\mathsf{Gr_{et}}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})_{\setminus W},\mathcal{H})
is an (unpointed) étale morphism
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
.

10.4.
Factorisation categories and the nerve theorem

Let
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
be the Kleisli category of the monad
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
.
By
[
3
, Proposition 2.5]
, the monad
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
together with the category
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
satisfy the conditions of the nerve theorem
[
3
, Theorem 1.10]
(that is,
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
has arities
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
), and therefore the nerve functor
N\colon\mathsf{CO}\to\mathsf{psh}({\Xi^{\times}})
is fully faithful
if certain categories – associated to morphisms in
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
– are connected.

Let
S_{*}
be a pointed graphical species in
\mathsf{Set}
,
\mathcal{G}
a graph in
\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
, and let
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
be a morphism in the Kleisli category of
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
. By the Yoneda lemma,

{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})\cong\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}(\mathcal{G},{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*})\cong{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*}(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{\mathrm{colim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}}{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*}(\mathcal{C})

where

{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*}(\mathcal{C})\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}S_{\lx@sectionsign}&\mathcal{C}\cong(\shortmid),\\
\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X})&\mathcal{C}\cong{\mathcal{C}_{X}}.\end{array}\right.

So,
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
is represented by pairs
(\mathbf{\Gamma},\alpha)
of

•

a non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}
with colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})
, viewed as a generic morphism
[\mathbf{\Gamma}]\in{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))={\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))
,

•

an element
\alpha\in S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))
viewed, by the Yoneda lemma, as a morphism
\alpha\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}({\Upsilon_{*}}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}),S_{*})

such that the induced composition
\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{[\mathbf{\Gamma}]}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\alpha}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))\twoheadrightarrow({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*})(\mathcal{G})
is precisely
\beta
.

Definition 10.14
.

Objects of the
factorisation category

\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
of
\beta
are pairs
(\mathbf{\Gamma},\alpha)
that represent
\beta
as above, and morphisms in
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)((\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1},\alpha^{1}),(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2},\alpha^{2}))
are commuting diagrams in
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}

(10.15)

\textstyle{{[\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1}]}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S_{*}}
\textstyle{{[\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2}]}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{2}}

such that
g
is a morphism in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
.

Lemma 10.16
.

For all pointed graphical species
S_{*}
in
\mathsf{Set}
, all graphs
\mathcal{G}\in\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
, and all
\beta\in\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}(\mathcal{G},{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S)
, the category
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
is connected.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{G}\cong(\shortmid)
, then
\mathbf{\Gamma}
is isomorphic to the identity
(\shortmid)
-shaped graph of graphs.
If
\mathcal{G}\cong{\mathcal{C}_{X}}
for some finite set
X
, and
\beta\in{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{X}\cong{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
, then objects of
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
have the form
(\mathcal{X},\alpha)
where
\mathcal{X}\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}
is an admissible (in general not connected)
X
-graph,
\alpha\in S_{*}(\mathcal{X})
and
\beta
is the image of
\alpha
under the universal morphism
S_{*}(\mathcal{X})\to({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*})_{X}
. Since
({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*})_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
, if
(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
also represents
\beta
, then
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
are connected in
X\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
and hence they are similar in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
.
Hence, if
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{C}
is in the image of
{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}_{*}\subset\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
, then
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
is connected for all
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{C},S_{*})\cong{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S(\mathcal{C})
.

For general
\mathcal{G}\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
, and
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
, observe first that, since colimits of graphs of graphs are computed componentwise, we may assume that
\mathcal{G}
is connected. Assume also that
\mathcal{G}\not\cong\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
. Let
(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{i},\alpha^{i})
,
i=1,2
be objects of
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
. Then, for each
({\mathcal{C}_{X}},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}
,
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1}(b)
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2}(b)
are in the same connected component of
X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
, since the lemma holds when
\mathcal{G}\cong{\mathcal{C}_{X}}
. Therefore,
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1}(\mathcal{G})
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2}(\mathcal{G})
are similar in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
.
∎

10.4
now follows from
[
3
, Sections 1 & 2]
.

Proof of
10.4
.

The induced nerve functor
N\colon\mathsf{CO}\to\mathsf{psh}({\Xi^{\times}})
is fully faithful by
10.16
,
[
3
, Proposition 2.5]
, and
[
3
, Propositions 1.5 & 1.9]
.

Moreover, by
[
3
, Theorem 1.10]
its essential image is the subcategory of those presheaves on
{\Xi^{\times}}
whose restriction to
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
are in the image of the fully faithful embedding
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{Gr}_{*})
. So, a presheaf
P
on
{\Xi^{\times}}
described the nerve of a circuit operad if and only if

P(\mathcal{G})\cong\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}_{*}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C})
, and hence, by
[
34
, Lemma 7.8]
(finality of
\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\subset\mathsf{el}_{*}{(\mathcal{G})}
, see also
[
30
, Section IX.3]
),

P(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C}).

Therefore, the Segal condition (
10.5
) is satisfied and
10.4
is proved.
∎

Remark 10.17
.

Let
S_{*}
be a pointed graphical species and
\mathcal{G}
a connected graph. A morphism
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
in the Kleisli category of
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
is represented by a pair
(\mathbf{\Gamma},\underline{\alpha})
of

•

a non-degenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of connected graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
with (connected) colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})
;

•

an element
\underline{\alpha}\in\widetilde{L}S_{*}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))
such that, for each
({\mathcal{C}_{X}},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))}
,
LS_{*}(b)(\underline{\alpha})
is given by a tuple
(\phi_{i})_{i=1}^{k}\in LS_{X}
(with
\phi_{i}\in S_{X_{i}}
, and
\coprod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}=X
) of elements of
S
.

Such pairs
(\mathbf{\Gamma},\underline{\alpha})
are the objects of the
connected factorisation category

\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)_{c}
of
\beta
. Morphisms in
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)_{c}((\mathbf{\Gamma},\underline{\alpha}),(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\prime},\underline{\alpha}^{\prime}))
are pointed morphisms
g\in\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}),\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\mathcal{G}))
of connected graphs, such that the following diagram commutes in
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
:

(10.18)

\textstyle{{\mathbf{\Gamma}}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S_{*}.}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\alpha}}

By
[
3
, Proposition 2.5]
, the full subcategory of
{\Xi^{\times}}
on the connected graphs induces a fully faithful nerve functor from
\mathsf{CO}
(i.e.
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
provides arities for
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
) if and only if
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)_{c}
is connected for all connected graphs
\mathcal{G}
, all pointed graphical species
S_{*}
, and all
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
.

To see that this is not the case,
let
S_{*}={\Upsilon_{*}}(\shortmid\amalg\shortmid)
, and let
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
. A
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
-shaped graph of connected graphs is just a connected graph
\mathcal{H}
with empty boundary. Recall (from
Section

9.1
) that
\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{H})}
denotes the category of non-degenerate
\mathcal{H}
-shaped graphs of disjoint unions of corollas. An element
\underline{\alpha}\in\widetilde{L}{\Upsilon_{*}}(\shortmid\amalg\shortmid)(\mathcal{H})
is just an
\mathcal{H}
-shaped graph of disjoint unions of corollas
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{H})}
with colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes}(\mathcal{H})\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
, followed by a morphism
f\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes}(\mathcal{H}),(\shortmid\amalg\shortmid))
.
In particular, each component of
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes}(\mathcal{H})
is either an isolated vertex, or of the form
\mathcal{L}^{k}
or
\mathcal{W}^{m}
(
k,m\geq 0
).

So, let
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}},(\shortmid\amalg\shortmid))
be the morphism represented by the unique
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
-shaped graph of graphs with colimit
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\amalg\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
, together with the componentwise morphism
(z\amalg z)\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\amalg\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}},\shortmid\amalg\shortmid)
.

Then
\beta
is also represented by
(\mathcal{H},\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes},f)
, illustrated in
Figure

22
, where

\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{4}}^{1\ddagger 2,3\ddagger 4}
is the graph with two loops at one vertex,
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes}
is given by
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{4}},b)\mapsto\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{2}}
with colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes}(\mathcal{H})=\mathcal{W}\amalg\mathcal{W}
, and
f=(\kappa\amalg\kappa)\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathcal{W}\amalg\mathcal{W},\shortmid\amalg\shortmid)
.

=
(z\amalg z)
(\kappa\amalg\kappa)
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\amalg\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes}
\mathcal{H}

Figure 22.
There is no zigzag of morphisms
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\leadsto\mathcal{H}
in
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
that connect
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\amalg\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}})
and
(\mathcal{H},\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\boxtimes})
in
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)_{c}
.

Since morphisms in
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
factor as vertex deletion morphisms followed by morphisms in
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}
, and the single vertex of
\mathcal{H}
has valency four,
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)_{c}
is connected if there is a morphism
of the form
ch_{e}\circ z\in\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr_{et}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}},\mathcal{H})
making (
10.18
) commute. But then the image of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
under the induced morphism
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\to\mathcal{H}\to(\shortmid\amalg\shortmid)
factors through an inclusion
(\shortmid)\hookrightarrow(\shortmid\amalg\shortmid)
, so the diagram (
10.18
) does not commute.

Remark 10.19
.

Both the nerve theorem
[
34
, Theorem 8.2]
for modular operads on which
10.4
is built, as well as Hackney, Robertson and Yau’s nerve theorem
[
15
, 3.6]
, lead to well-defined models of
(\infty,1)
-modular operads in which the fibrant objects in the appropriate category of presheaves are precisely those that satisfy the weak Segal condition: in the case of
[
34
]
it followed from the results of
[
7
]
that these were given by
P\colon\Xi^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{sSet}
such that, for all (connected) graphs
\mathcal{G}\in\Xi
,

P(\mathcal{G})\simeq\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C}).

However, describing a similar model for circuit operads (algebras) is more challenging. The presence of unconnected graphs means that, neither the method of
[
15
]
, nor that of
[
34
, Corollary 8.14]
may be directly extended to a model structure on functors from
{{\Xi^{\times}}}^{\mathrm{op}}
(or some subcategory thereof) to
\mathsf{sSet}
that gives a good description of weak circuit operads. For this reason, a description of a model for
(\infty,1)
-circuit operads is deferred to a future work.
